## Document 006: 10月会合で日銀は政策金利を据え置き 2％の物価安定に向け利上げ継続へ

Report source: DIR

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026年 10月 30日 全5頁

10月会合で日銀は政策金利を据え置き 
2％の物価安定に向け利上げ継続へ 
利上げの悪影響は今のところ限定的、12月に1.5％へ追加利上げか

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 秋元 虹輝

[要約] 
◼ 日本銀行（日銀）は2026年10月29、30日の金融政策決定会合で、短期金利を1.25％
程度に据え置いた。日銀の政策運営は、基調的な物価上昇率を「2％に近づける」局面
から「2％程度で安定させる」局面に移った。今後は、経済・物価・金融情勢に応じて
政策金利を引き上げ、金融緩和の度合いを調整していく方針だ。 
◼ 日銀は2024年 3月以降、金融政策の正常化を進めてきたが、企業・家計への悪影響は
これまでのところ限定的だ。企業では比較的良好な資金調達環境やネット有利子負債
比率の低下が、家計では収入の増加や借入返済額の減少に加え、住宅ローン金利上昇
の返済額への反映の遅れが、足元の負担増を抑えている。ただし、家計の借入残高は
収入対比で高水準にあり、金利高が長期化すれば、返済負担が徐々に高まる可能性が
ある。 
◼ 当社では、日銀は 2026年 12月会合で短期金利を1.5％、2027年 3月会合で 1.75％へ
引き上げた後、いったん据え置くとみている。ただし、その後も金融環境がなお緩和
的で物価上振れリスクが残れば、同年6月会合で2.0％への追加利上げに踏み切る可能
性がある。

2026年 10月会合で日銀は政策金利の据え置きを決定 
日本銀行（日銀）は2026年10月29、30日に開催された金融政策決定会合で、無担保コール
レート・オーバーナイト物（以下、短期金利）を 1.25％程度に据え置くことを決めた1。今回
は利上げを決定した翌月の会合ということもあり、これまでの利上げの影響を見極めることを
重視したとみられる。

1 日本銀行「当面の金融政策運営について」（2026年10月 30日）

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

連続利上げに慎重な姿勢は、前回会合後の総裁発言からもうかがえる。9月会合直後の記者
会見で植田和男総裁は、「大きな幅の利上げとか何回も連続して利上げするというようなこと
は、端的に申し上げれば、物価が目標を超えてものすごい上がっていくというリスクが高いか、
あるいは既に目標を超えちゃっているかというようなことに依存するんだと思います」2と述べ、
その例として2022、23年の米欧の高インフレ局面を挙げた。 
また、日銀が保有する上場投資信託（ETF）については、2026年 1月以降の売却が市場に大
きな影響を与えることなく円滑に進捗したことなどを踏まえ、2027年 1月から年間の売却ペー
スを簿価で 3,300億円程度から 8,600億円程度（市場全体の売買代金に占める年間売却額（時
価）の割合は0.05％程度から0.1％程度）に引き上げることを決めた3。

インフレ基調は2％目標と概ね整合的で、今後は定着に向けて利上げ継続へ 
9月会合の声明文で「2％に近づいている」4と評価した消費者物価の基調的な上昇率につい
ては、10月会合で公表された「経済・物価情勢の展望」（展望レポート）で「2％の『物価安定
の目標』と概ね整合的な水準」に修正された。さらに、「従来は、基調的な物価上昇率を 2％に
向けて引き上げていく過程にあり、物価の下振れリスクに相応の注意を払うことが必要であっ
たが、足もとでは局面が変化しており、今後は、基調的な物価上昇率が 2％を超えて上振れて
いくリスクが顕在化し、それがその後の経済に悪影響を及ぼすことがないよう、十分に留意す
る必要がある」と指摘した5。 
植田総裁は 9月会合直後の記者会見ですでに、基調的な物価上昇率を 2％程度で安定させて
いくことが重要になったとして、 「政策の局面が変化した」と説明していた。10月会合での文
言変更は、こうした政策局面の変化をより明確にしたものといえる。また、10月 16日の記者
会見でも、今後は基調的な物価上昇率が「これ以上上がるということは容認できない」6と述べ、
2％近辺での定着を目指す考えを改めて示した。 
展望レポートでは、引き続き物価上振れリスクへの警戒を示し、経済・物価・金融情勢に応
じて政策金利を引き上げ、金融緩和の度合いを調整していく方針を前回と同様に明記した。日
銀が物価上振れリスクを見極める上で注視しているのが、①中東情勢に伴う原油価格、②AI関
連需要、③為替相場の3つである。足元ではAI関連投資の収益性への懸念が広がり、円安も一
服しているものの、日銀はこれらの動向が国内物価にどの程度波及するかを引き続き見極めて
いくとみられる。 
展望レポートを見ると、2026年度の実質 GDP成長率は春先以降のエネルギー・原材料価格の
上昇を受けて伸び率を縮小しつつも緩やかな成長を続け、2027年度以降は所得から支出への前

2 日本銀行「総裁記者会見」（2026年9月18日、24日公表） 
3 日本銀行「ETFの売却ペースの見直しに関する決定（2026年 10月）」（2026年 10月30日） 
4 日本銀行「金融市場調節方針の変更について」（2026年9月 18日） 
5 日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望（2026年10月）」（基本的見解、2026年10月 30日） 
6 日本銀行「植田総裁記者会見(10月 16日) ―G20終了後の植田総裁、三村財務官 共同記者会見における総裁
発言」（2026年10月 20日）

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

向きな循環メカニズムが強まるもとで成長率を緩やかに高めていくとされた（政策委員見通し
の中央値は2026年度＋0.7％、27年度＋0.7％、28年度＋0.8％） 。 
一方、消費者物価（除く生鮮食品）の前年比は、原油価格の上昇や半導体価格等の上昇、為
替円安などを背景に見通し期間前半に 2％をはっきりと上回る水準まで伸び率を高めた後、後
半には原油高の影響の減衰により、 2％程度に向けてプラス幅を縮小する見通しだ（政策委員
見通しの中央値は 2026年度＋2.5％、2027年度＋2.6％、2028年度＋2.0％） 。夏場以降の原油
価格上昇の影響などから、2027年度が前回（7月）から 0.2％pt上方修正された。消費者物価
（除く生鮮食品・エネルギー）の見通しは 2026年度＋2.4％、2027年度＋2.7％、2028年度＋
2.3％と、前回からの修正は小幅にとどまった。なお、2027年 4月から実施される可能性のあ
る飲食料品の消費税率引き下げは、物価見通しに反映されていない。 
利上げが続く中でも企業・家計への影響はこれまでのところ限定的 
日銀は2024年3月の金融政策決定会合でマイナス金利政策の解除とイールドカーブ・コント
ロール（長短金利操作）の撤廃を決め、その後 5回の利上げや国債買入れの減額を実施するな
ど金融政策の正常化を進めてきた。 
もっとも、企業や家計の経済活動への悪影響はこれまでのところ限定的で、金融環境はなお
緩和的とみられる。まず企業部門を見ると、日銀短観の業況判断DIは高水準を維持しており、
資金繰り判断DIや金融機関の貸出態度判断DIにも目立った悪化は見られない（図表1左）。中
小企業でも顕著な悪化は見られず、利上げが進む中でも、企業の景況感や資金調達環境は総じ
て良好な状態にある。 
ただし、過去の利上げ局面との単純な比較には注意が必要だ。1990年頃までの利上げは、資
産バブル期を含む高成長・高インフレ環境の下で実施されたのに対し、 2000年代以降は低成
長・デフレないし低インフレが長く続き、企業の財務構造も大きく変化した。足元で利上げの
影響が表れにくい背景には、企業の財務体質の改善がある。 
有利子負債から現預金を控除した額の総資産に対する比率（ネット有利子負債比率）は、資
本金 1千万円以上 10億円未満の中小・中堅企業で歴史的な低水準にある（図表 1右）。大企業
では中小・中堅企業ほど低下しておらず、資本金 1千万円未満の企業では相対的に高いものの、
企業部門全体では 1970～90 年代に比べて大幅に低下している。こうした財務構造の変化によ
り、政策金利が上昇しても、企業部門全体では利払い費の増加を通じた金融引き締めの影響が
以前より表れにくくなっていると考えられる。

図表 1：日銀短観の各種 DIの長期推移（左） 、企業規模別に見たネット有利子負債比率（右）

（注）左図は全産業ベースで、破線で示した中小企業は資本金 2千万円以上 1億円未満。 「利上げ局面」は利上
げ開始から利下げ直前までの期間。右図のネット有利子負債比率は、有利子負債から現預金を控除して総資産
で除したもの。資本金 1千万円未満のデータは年度値の平均（その他は四半期値の平均） 。 
（出所）日本銀行、財務省より大和総研作成

家計部門についても、これまでのところ金利上昇の影響は限定的だ。ただし、ストック面か
ら金利上昇への耐性を見ると、借入残高対年収比率は 2000年代初めから大幅に上昇している
（図表 2左）。データが遡及可能な2002年と比べると、2026年上半期の同比率は約53％pt高い。
要因別では、借入残高の増加が同比率を約 66％pt押し上げた一方、年収の増加が約 12％pt押
し下げた。家計の借入負担はストック面で以前より重くなっており、金利上昇が長期化すれば、
その影響が徐々に顕在化する可能性がある。 
図表 2：家計の借入残高対年収比率（左） 、借入返済負担率（右）の2002年からの変化幅

（注）二人以上の勤労者世帯。左図の借入残高対年収比率は、1 世帯あたりの借入残高（「住宅・土地のための
負債」と「住宅・土地以外の負債」の合計）を年間世帯収入で除したもの。右図の借入返済負担率は 1世帯あ
たりの実収入に対する借金返済額の比率。「低所得世帯」は年間収入五分位階級のうち第Ⅰ・Ⅱ分位、「高所得
世帯」は第Ⅳ・Ⅴ分位を集計。「返済世帯比率要因」は、住宅ローン返済世帯の収入が二人以上の勤労者世帯
全体の収入に占める割合の変化による寄与を表す。 
（出所）総務省より大和総研作成

### Page 5

1

5 / 5

一方、フロー面から足元の資金繰りを見ると、収入に対する借入返済額の比率（借入返済負
担率）は、返済額の減少や収入の増加を背景に、2002年以降の低水準にある（図表2右）。2026
年上半期の借入返済負担率は2002年を約1.1％pt下回った。要因別では、住宅ローン返済世帯
の収入シェア（住宅ローン返済世帯の収入が二人以上の勤労者世帯全体の収入に占める割合）
の上昇が負担率を押し上げた一方、返済額の減少と収入の増加が押し下げた。所得階級別に見
ると、足元では低所得世帯（年収下位 4割）の返済負担率が相対的に上昇しているものの、低
所得世帯、高所得世帯（年収上位4割）ともに2002年の水準を下回る。このため、金利上昇が
家計の資金繰りを大きく圧迫する状況には至っていない。 
ただし、足元の返済負担の低さは今後も続くとは限らない。変動金利型住宅ローンでは、毎
月の返済額の急増を抑える「5 年ルール」や「125％ルール」が広く採用されている7。こうし
た仕組みは、金利上昇の影響が返済額に反映される時期を遅らせ、短期的な負担増を和らげる
半面、負担の顕在化を将来に先送りする。借入残高が高水準にあることも踏まえると、金利高
が長期化するほど家計の返済負担が高まる可能性には留意が必要だ。

中立金利を探りながら、日銀は段階的な利上げを継続へ 
当社では、日銀は次回 2026年 12月の金融政策決定会合で短期金利を1.5％に、2027年 3月
会合で 1.75％に引き上げるとみている。その後は経済・物価・金融情勢への影響を見極めるた
め、いったん政策金利を据え置くと予想する。ただし、1.75％への引き上げ後も金融環境がな
お緩和的で、物価上振れリスクが残る場合には、同年 6月会合で 2.0％への追加利上げに踏み
切る可能性がある。 
日銀は2026年3月に公表したレポート8で、6つのモデルによる自然利子率の推計レンジを▲
0.9％程度～＋0.5％程度と示した。2％の物価上昇率を想定すると、名目の中立金利は 1.1～
2.5％程度で、1.75％はそのレンジのほぼ中央に位置する。だが、中立金利を正確に推計する
ことは困難で、その推計レンジには大きな幅がある9。そのため日銀は、各種データでこれまで
の利上げの影響や物価の基調などを確認しながら、金融緩和の度合いを徐々に調整していくと
みられる。

7 5年ルールは、変動金利型住宅ローンの金利が変動しても、毎月の返済額を5年間据え置く仕組み。125％ル
ールは、返済額の見直し時に、新たな返済額の上限を従来の 125％とする仕組み。いずれも法令で一律に定め
られたものではなく、適用の有無は金融機関や商品によって異なる。 
8 日本銀行「自然利子率の動向と金融緩和度合いの評価」（「日銀レビュー」、2026-J-5、2026年3月） 
9 2026年6月会合後の記者会見で内田眞一副総裁は、中立金利の推計値には「相当なばらつき」があるとした
上で、「短期金利を上げていく過程で、それを含めた金融環境の変化ということを点検して、中立金利の水準
を探りながら、金融緩和の度合いを調整していく」と述べた（日本銀行「総裁記者会見（内田副総裁代理）」
（2026年6月16日、17日公表））。

## Document 013: 115年9月海關進出口貿易初步統計 (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/115年9月海關進出口貿易初步統計 (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: MOF
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

2 
二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注， 9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)， 其中電腦及其附屬單元增157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
1. 第 3 季出口較上年同季增 755.9 億美元(+44.6%)，以資通與視聽產
品增 367.6 億美元(+55.0%)、電子零組件增 308.4 億美元(+51.2%)
增加較多。 
2. 累計 1 至 9 月出口較上年同期增 2,089.8 億美元(+46.2%)，其中資
通與視聽產品增 1,222.2 億美元(+72.4%)、電子零組件增 726.6 億
美元(+45.7%)。

等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口440.5 億美元， 較上年同月增163.9億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元， 較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、 電子零組件增10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)， 對東協出口1,339.6 億美元 ， 增463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元 ， 增138.4 億美元(+48.8%)， 對日本出口277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月自中國大陸與香港進口 135.0 億美元 ， 較上年同月增50.1 億美元
(+59.1%)，自南韓進口 116.3 億美元，增52.6 億美元(+82.5%)，自東
協進口 106.0 億美元，增 52.6 億美元(+98.4%)，自日本進口 64.3 億
美元，增 17.7 億美元(+38.0%)，自歐洲進口 50.2 億美元，增 3.5 億
美元(+7.4%)，自美國進口 48.1 億美元，增 6.5 億美元(+15.5%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增77.6%、
自南韓增 65.8%、自中國大陸與香港增 42.8%、自日本增 25.7%、自
美國增 21.4%、自歐洲增 10.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）主要國家/地區今年 1-8 月外貿景氣普遍走升，以南韓(1-9 月)出口年
增 56.8%最為強勁，香港、新加坡亦分別年增 49.0%、39.8%；中國
大陸、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-7 月)、日本各增
9.7%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-8 月以香港進口年增 50.5%較
突出，新加坡、中國大陸分別年增 37.3%、27.0%，南韓(1-9 月)、德
國(1-7 月)分別增 19.7%、10.0%，日本、美國各增 7.8%、4.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季 、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口635.9 億美元，年增51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、 交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加 ；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄 ， 分別年增59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體 經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用 持續深化，國際雲端服務業者 及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆， 均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

## Document 103: 261027_ml_faraday

### Page 1

Faraday

Valuation remains stretched; timely 
revenue delivery to be key watchpoint

Reiterate Rating: UNDERPERFORM | PO: 120.00 TWD | Price: 191.50 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on record MCU mass production revenue  
Faraday’s 3Q26 sales was +11% QoQ, above BofAe/street of -7%/-3% QoQ and its 
guidance of -single-digit% QoQ, mainly driven by strong MP growth (+19.6% QoQ) on 
record-high MCU sales and accelerating ASIC shipments, coupled with continued IP 
revenue strength more than offsetting deferred NRE revenue recognition. 3Q26 GM 
dropped by 270 bps to 42.5% due to product mix, while increased Opex from salary 
adjustments brought 3Q26 EPS to NT$1.07 (BofAe/Street NT$1.27/0.66).  
IP and NRE strength support 4Q26 margin improvement 
4Q26 sales is guided to remain flat QoQ, as growth in IP and NRE revenue offset decline 
in MP revenue on lower MCU shipments following a high base in 3Q26. With sustained 
strength in IP segment underpinned by higher wafer pricing and utilization, along with 
NRE milestone delivery, 4Q26 GM should edge up to 43.7% on better mix, in our view.  
High-end MCU and advanced projects drive 2027 growth  
We now forecast Faraday's 2026/2027 sales to -27%/+36% YoY, supported by healthy 
demand for high-end MCU and gradual ramp in advanced projects. While the design 
change from 4nm to 2nm for a US customer’s ASIC project should bring greater contract 
value, Faraday’s capability to deliver on schedule and achieve timely revenue recognition 
remains key watchpoint. On higher advanced packaging/nodes projects contribution 
through 2027, GM should decline slightly from 44.5% in 2026 to 42.9% in 2027. 
Reiterate Underperform with unchanged NT$120 PO 
We lower our 2026 EPS from NT$4.05 to NT$3.19 on higher Opex and trim 2027-28 
EPS by 3-10% as we remain cautious on the timing of NRE/MP recognition for advanced 
projects given the increased design complexity and constrained OSAT capacity. We 
reiterate Underperform rating with unchanged PO of NT$120 on 17x 2027E P/E. Current 
valuation appears stretched with shares trading at 28x '27 P/E (vs. historical 13-42x).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Faraday Technology Corp. was established in 1993 and 
listed in 1999. The company is an affiliate of the second 
biggest semiconductor logic foundry (UMC) in Taiwan, and 
has been the main provider to UMC's IP library. Faraday is 
also known for its total solution in ASIC (customized chips) 
which includes chip-design and turnkey mass production, 
on top of its IP offering. The company has a diversified end-
market exposure, including internet of things, factory 
automation, solar, microcontrollers etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have an Underperform rating on Faraday, as we are 
wary of the slower for longer business outlook in mature 
node projects, while its collaboration with non-TSMC 
foundries bears the uncertainty.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Faraday (FDYTF) 
Our PO NT$120 is derived from 17x 2027E P/E, which is at low-to-mid-end of the 
company's historical valuation range. In our view, the multiple is backed by gross margin 
expansion from 27% in 2025 to over 40% in 2026/27 while revenue would slow down 
from a peak in 2025.

Upside risks: (1) faster-than-expected progress in new businesses including advanced 
nodes and advanced packaging, and (2) a better margin profile thanks to more stringent 
opex and enhanced value-add to customers

Downside risks: (1) downturn in foundry business cycle, given that the firm adopts a 
cost-plus model for turnkey (mass production) business, and (2) slower qualification and 
ramp-up of the yield rate for advanced packaging business

Faraday Technology
New opportunities in advanced nodes
Q326 beat on turnkey strength and non-op gains; Q426 outlook positive
Q326 EPS of NT$1.07 came in above UBS/Street expectations mainly on  non-op gains.
Q326 sales came in at NT$3.66 bn (+11% QoQ), ahead of its original guidance but in
line with its comment  during Taiwan Summit with turnkey sales up 20% QoQ given
better support from OSAT and foundries, despite NRE declining 31% QoQ. Q326 GM

decreased 2.7ppt QoQ to 42.5% on higher turnkey mix, while  OPEX grew 9% QoQ  on
salary adjustment and higher bonus accrual. Non-op gain of NT$266mn mainly includes
valuation gains on an investee company. For Q426F, it guided sales  flat  QoQ remaining
at high levels with IP and NRE up QoQ, and turnkey down QoQ. GM is guided back to
~45%, reflecting normalized ASIC economics; OPEX should decline single digit QoQ.
2027 growth outlook supported by AI, advanced packaging and 12nm node

Management guided for double-digit sales growth in 2027, with all three businesses, IP ,
NRE and turnkey, expected to grow, driven by three key catalysts: AI, advanced
packaging and new UMC 12nm project contribution. For IP , growth should be
supported by higher royalties on foundry price hikes/customer capacity expansion and
initial  contribution from 12nm node; NRE should benefit from 2nm/4nm projects
entering peak recognition, with 3-4 ASIC tape-outs targeted in H127; while MP should

see growth in both MCU and ASIC as new projects gradually ramp. AI-related mix was at
8-9% of Q326 sales, and is expected to increase throughout 2027 alongside
accelerating advanced-packaging shipments. We expect 2027E sales to grow 37% YoY.
2nm design win opens new advanced-node and product opportunities
Faraday secured a 2nm GAA special purpose  ASIC project worth several tens of millions
of US dollars in NRE, which management described as its largest-ever design win and

most advanced project to date. The project spans front-end design and IP integration,
back-end design, customized 3D DRAM and advanced packaging, and marks Faraday's
first customized 3D DRAM and hybrid-bonding project. It expects to recognize its first
NRE milestone  in H127, followed by MP targeted within 1-2 years. Beyond 2nm, Faraday
is expanding into new applications including OCS, CXL and Si-cap, leveraging its
capabilities in advanced packaging, interposer design and ASIC integration. We believe

successful execution by Faraday and its foundry partner could strengthen Faraday's
positioning in advanced-node projects and create follow-on design-win opportunities.
Valuation: Raise PT to NT$200, stay Neutral
We raise    2026E EPS (reported) from NT$3.43 to NT$3.68 on better Q326, but lower
2027/28E EPS by 4%/1% to factor in softer CE demand and higher OPEX. We raise our
PT to NT$200 from NT$190 using 24x 2027E PE (vs prior 22x). We raise our multiple as

the recent 2nm project win should boost investor confidence, but stay Neutral as the
advanced nodes (4nm/2nm) turnkey revenue recognition depends on the foundry
partner's execution. We await clearer evidence before revisiting our assumptions.

Company Description
Faraday was founded in 1993, spun off from UMC's IP and semiconductor design service
department as the first IC design service company in Asia. It is an IC design and production
service provider that initially focused on specialty applications on mature nodes by partnering

with UMC and gradually upgraded to advanced nodes through working with Samsung. The
company had its IPO on the Taiwan Securities Exchange in 1999.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Faraday using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced nodes migration, further delay of
advanced packaging ramp, and growing competition in specialty projects from China IC
design service companies.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 129: 261029_daiwa_ASE

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_ASE.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: ASE disclosed a strong set of quarterly results and guidance 
on 29 October, both beating market expectations, thanks to the strong AI 
scaling which is just getting stronger to benefit ASE’s leading-edge 
advanced packaging (LEAP) business. We raise our forecasts and 12M TP 
and reaffirm our Outperform (2) call on the stock; accumulate into any 
technical pullback.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. ASE reported 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD27bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 25%/18% 
thanks to higher semiconductor assembly & test (SAT, or IC ATM by 
company definition) revenue which came in at TWD151bn (USD4.7bn), 7% 
above our estimate, for higher gross margin (GM) of 30.5%, 1.9pp above 
ours and topping ASE’s structural target of 24-30%. The strength was 
amplified further by lower non-operating losses, resulting in the strong 
bottom-line beat (table on p.2). Guidance was another beat thanks to the 
strong SAT business where ASE expects revenue to grow 11-13% QoQ for 
GM to top its record of 30.5% during the COVID upturn. We raise our 2026-
28E EPS by 14-24% assuming its SAT GM to reach 33% through 2027.

Strong AI gets stronger. Discussions centred on ASE’s LEAP business, 
where management further hiked revenue target this year beyond USD4bn 
(USD1.6bn in 2025), after 3 hikes YTD, thanks to strong demand for its on-
substrate (oS) packaging and initial ramp of its full-process solutions. With 
addition of new co-packaged optics (CPO) solutions, ASE expects the 
LEAP revenue to double YoY in 2027. As LEAP is margin-accretive, ASE 
expects the SAT GM to continue improving beyond 4Q26, making the 
ceiling of its structural GM target now as the floor to kick off 2027 for a 
strong start. Given management’s emphasis on “friendly pricing”, we expect 
likely continued cost passthrough across the supply chain to add to GM 
upside. Working hard to narrow the supply/demand gap, ASE further raised 
this year’s capex to USD11.5bn (from USD10.5bn), reinforcing our view 
that AI is scaling strongly to accelerate the chip industry growth.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for ASE to TWD710 (previous: TWD550), based on our revised ROE-
adjusted PBR target of 5.8x (previous: 5.2x) 2027E BVPS due to a higher 
ROE trajectory. Our revised TP translates into 24x 4-quarter forward PER, 
which we see as justifying the rich PBR target given its 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 25%. We reiterate our Outperform (2) call with key downside risks 
of any “silicon inflation” and geopolitical overhang leading to AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 5-10% above the consensus as we 
believe the street has yet to factor in its stronger LEAP business.

29 October 2026

Entering an even stronger earnings cycle (III)

➢ 3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance all beat expectations 
➢ Strong AI scaling is getting even stronger to benefit LEAP 
➢ Reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD710

Source: Daiwa forecasts

Share price performance

Company profile 
Established in April 2018, ASE Industrial Holding Co (HoldCo) is a holding company which privatized 
ASE and SPIL fully on 30 April 2018 through equity and cash swap, respectively, and listed on the same 
day with senior management assigned mainly by ASE. HoldCo has become the strong No.1 OSAT 
maker in the world, offering a comprehensive product portfolio to its customers from wirebonding, flip-
chip & bumping, system-in-package (SiP) to broad-based electronics manufacturing services (EMS).

### Page 5

5

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
ASE’s Board of Directors consists of nine members with diverse professional backgrounds.
Three of the directors are independent, representing 33 percent of the board. The Chairman also
serves as the Chief Executive Officer, resulting in unified leadership and centralized decision-
making. To maintain effective governance and oversight under this structure, ASE relies on its
independent directors and established board processes. The board currently includes one
female director, and the company continues to pursue long-term goals related to board diversity
and balanced representation. ASE has an Audit Committee, Compensation Committee, Risk
Management Committee, and Chief Government Officer to conduct effective supervision.
Capital management 3
In the past 5 years, ASE paid over 50% or nearly 50% of its earnings in dividends. However, the
dividend trend was volatile in dollar and payout ratio terms. We forecast its dividend payout ratio
to average over 50% in the next 2 years.
Related party &
transaction 2
ASE undertakes related-party transactions with its subsidiaries on sales and purchases. The
total related-party transaction sales/purchase amounts were less than 10% of its total
sales/purchases, and the transaction terms with related parties were normal compared with
those with other customers/suppliers. Thus, we do not think related-party transactions have a
meaningful impact on overall operations.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
The supply of raw materials has the most direct impact on ASE's operations and manufacturing.
To lower overall supply-chain risks, ASE has expanded the scope of sustainability risk
management to non-tier-1 suppliers. ASE currently has over 600 non-tier-1 suppliers.
ASE requires its suppliers to comply with its Supplier Code of Conduct, which aims to ensure
that suppliers conduct business in a manner that meets labour, health & safety, environment,
business ethics & management, and various corporate compliance standards.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
ASE has adopted 3 water-use strategies of reduce, reuse and recycle. Through its water
management measures, it reduced water consumption density (water consumption/revenue) by
43% in 2024 vs. that of 2015. ASE is committed to increasing water reuse at its facilities in order
to improve water efficiency.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
ASE is committed to improving waste recycling and reuse through the 5 key elements of a
circular economy of: direct recycling, reuse, off-site regeneration, renewable materials, and
supply and rental. The recycling rate of general waste was 90% in 2024 and its hazardous waste
density declined by 53% in 2024 vs. that of 2015.
E GHG emissions 1
ASE is committed to achieving net zero emissions by advancing low carbon energy adoption,
developing smart grid technologies, and strengthening high tech energy management systems.
As of 2024, 19% of total manufacturing energy consumption comes from renewable sources,
88% of ASE facilities use renewable energy, and 10 sites have achieved RE100 certification.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

投資建議 
三大事業部同步成長，以及歐洲、東南亞、
印度與中國等非北美市場大幅貢獻的帶動
下，預期 2027 年仍將延續五成的強勁成長
動能，營運獲利可創新高。合庫預估 2026 / 
2027 年獲利成長 156% / 72%，稅後每股盈
餘 39.73 / 68.33 元，建議買進，目標價 820 
元 (2027 PER 12x)。 
業務營收比重

公司簡介  
營邦企業股份有限公司創立於 1996 年 ， 為全
球伺服器與儲存系統製造商領導者之一。 產品
發展方向由工業電腦延伸至系統散熱解決方
案，再擴展至高密度系統解決方案。近年來，
營邦深耕企業級伺服器與儲存解決方案領域，
專注於高效能運算 、 資安應用及資料中心基礎
架構之產品研發與製造，產品範疇涵蓋類別主
要包含：伺服器機箱、存儲系統、系統平台、
產業設備等產品。

2

營運展望 
1. 2H26 營運動能來自 AMD Helios 伺服器專案開始放量：針對 Meta、Oracle 與 
OpenAI 等客戶的 AMD Helios 專案，已由去年的研發與小量送樣階段，於今
年正式啟動大量生產。儘管受限於保密協議無法透露單一客戶細節，但整體專
案規模與出貨量將迎來顯著成長。2026 年上半年營收約 41.0 億元，第三季單
季貢獻即達 40.3 億元 ， 推升前三季累計營收至 81.3 億元 。 受惠下半年強勁拉
貨，全年營收預估將突破百億大關並創下歷史新高，年增率上看五成。 
2. 與 NVIDIA 深化儲存專案合作，共同開發下次世代應用：跟 NVIDIA 除延續
多年來在人工智慧資料處理器 (Data Processing Unit, DPU AI) 儲存市場合作，
更於今年  GTC 大會深化與  NVIDIA 策略夥伴關係。雙方正緊密圍繞最新  
Vera Rubin 平台，共同開發下一代鍵值快取 (Knowledge Base Cache, KB Cache)  
在內容管理系統 (Content Management System , CMS ) 的應用。與 NVIDIA 次
世代合作案正處聯合開發階段，將聚焦提供高階 AI 儲存完整解決方案，其市
場份額與營收貢獻將隨 NVIDIA 的市場推廣逐步發酵。 
3. 2027 年既有業務穩健成長 ， 新興 AI 業務加速拓展：在三大事業部同步成長 ，
以及歐洲 、 東南亞 、 印度與中國等非北美市場大幅貢獻的帶動下 ， 預期 2027 年
仍將延續五成的強勁營收成長幅度。預期標準品、AI 儲存與 CSP 三大事業群
的營收年增率將呈現均衡且穩定成長。其中，標準品於各區域市場皆持續貢獻
穩定營收，AI 儲存受惠於整體應用規模擴增趨勢而由專責團隊推動成長，CSP 
專案則依既有進度維持穩健的營收佔比 。公司整體毛利率穩定維持在 25 ~ 30% 
區間，未來各項產品銷售與營運規劃，也將持續以穩住此毛利率水準作為首要
目標。 
4. 三大事業部門業務能見度佳，明年營運獲利可創新高：三大事業部同步成長，
Server & Storage 標準品業績規模持續擴大，出貨量顯著成長。AI Storage 出貨
快速攀升，掌握重點客戶成長商機。CSP 專案持續拓展，AMD Helios 進入大
規模量產階段 。合庫預估 2026 年營收 122.8 億元 、+63.4% YoY， 毛利率 28.2%。
營業利益 18.7 億元、+112.8% YoY，營業利益率 15.2%，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 
17.1 億元，每股盈餘 39.73 元。 
5. ESG (營邦在 2026 年上櫃公司治理評鑑結果為 81 ~ 100%)：為實踐企業社會
責任，並促進經濟、社會與環境生態之平衡及永續發展，營邦制定「永續發展
實務守則」 與 「永續資訊管理作業程序」 ，將永續做為公司營運決策的一環。透
過檢視治理、環境與社會的管理架構，營邦運用永續策略促進創新，以符合平
衡環境、社會及公司治理發展之國際趨勢，並透過企業公民擔當，提升國家經
濟貢獻 ， 改善員工 、 社區 、 社會之生活品質 ， 促進以永續發展為本的競爭優勢 。

## Document 247: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일

## Document 254: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

## Document 327: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Eurozone: Marked increase in inflation in September 2026,
to a 3-year high
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022. Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.
Eurozone: Inflation (incl. & excl. Energy)

At the start of a very busy week for FX markets, the dollar is
largely holding onto recent gains. In focus are the US midterms,
where betting markets attach a 70% probability to a clean

updates on the US jobs market this week and are expecting

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
The US midterm elections are set to dominate a busy week in FX. A clean
sweep for the Democrats is expected, but the macro story can keep the dollar
supported
USD: Contemplating the midterms
The dollar is fractionally lower at the start of the week. It may be
taking its cue from oil prices, which are oﬀ 2-3% on reports that US
President Donald Trump wants to let diplomacy run its course with
Iran. That reverses Friday's sentiment, where some had positioned
for a fresh attack this weekend. Please see our latest thoughts on
the oil market here.

In focus this week are the US midterm elections. Betting websites
give the Democrats a 70-72% chance of taking both the House and
the Senate. One could make the case that the dollar should come
lower on such an outcome, since it would likely lead to gridlock on

could encourage President Trump to pursue an even more activist
foreign policy agenda. Many of those views are speculative,
however. What is not speculative is the US economy posting a

strong 3.4% quarter-on-quarter annualised growth rate in the third
quarter, inﬂation remaining above target for a sixth year in a row,
and the Federal Reserve readying to tighten again.
Feeding into the US macro debate this week will be ISM business
surveys and jobs data. Business surveys are expected to show
continued optimism, along with high price pressures. And Friday's
October jobs report is expected to show a modest rebound after a
soft September ﬁgure. Barring a wholly disastrous report on Friday,

we doubt the jobs data really moves the needle on the Fed
tightening story. Price data is more important here.
There will be continued focus on the tech sell-oﬀ, however. Apple
reports after the market close today. The 9% Nasdaq sell-oﬀ last
month has driven a softening in short-dated US rates, and any
further large sell-oﬀ in this sector is probably the biggest risk to
long dollar positioning.
Expect DXY to continue trading around 102.00, and we do not see a

strong case for a correction lower.

## Document 352: Industrials_ AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders

Source PDF: all report pdf/Industrials_ AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Industrials: AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders
Date:    5 hours ago
Industrials: AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Industrials: AI Jitters Versus
Booming Orders
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 28 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ant Rozetsky / Unsplash

I. AI Still Boosting Capital Goods Orders
The AI investment boom has been a double-edged sword for S&P 500
Industrials this year. Back in July, we noted that the sector was the
second-best performer among the S&P 500's 11 sectors, with a 17.7% ytd
gain, as investors favored companies supplying the equipment and
infrastructure needed for the AI buildout. Since then, however, intensifying

### Page 2

AI jitters have weighed on industrial stocks, fueled by a potentially toxic
trifecta of financial constraints: elevated borrowing costs, uncertain
investment returns, and concerns about whether sufficient capital will be
available to finance everyone's ambitious AI spending plans.
The corresponding reversal has been significant. As of October 27,
Industrials was up just 8.2% ytd, compared with a 12.8% gain for the S&P
500, slipping to sixth place among the 11 sectors (chart).

The sector's stock price index is trading below its 200-day moving average
but remains on a solid multi-year uptrend (chart).
Performance within the sector has been uneven so far this year, with rail
transportation leading, while industrial machinery and aerospace and
defense lagging (chart).

### Page 3

We think the recent selloff is overdone and continue to recommend
overweighting Industrials. For starters, the sector's earnings outlook
remains upbeat. Forward earnings rose 13.6% ytd to a record high last
week (chart).
The sector's forward profit margin is also at a record high of 11.7% (chart).

### Page 4

Analysts' net revisions of their revenues and earnings estimates have also
improved significantly in recent months, with both remaining historically
elevated (chart).
Furthermore, analysts still expect double-digit earnings growth of 17.6% in
2026 and 16.5% in 2027 (chart).

### Page 5

The sector's forward P/E has fallen sharply recently, but remains above
the overall market's valuation multiple (chart).
The AI investment boom continues despite the stock market's jitters about
the AI trade. The five major hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet, Microsoft,
Meta, and Oracle) are projected to spend nearly $800 billion on capex this
year and more than $1 trillion in 2027. So far this week, there has been
roughly $235 billion in announced AI financing, including up to $95 billion

from Meta, a $40 billion chip-and-server leasing arrangement involving
SpaceX, and a potential $100 billion IPO by Anthropic. We therefore
continue to recommend overweighting Industrials, which should continue
to benefit from the buildout.

### Page 6

Indeed, the AI spending boom is still boosting industrial activity, with much
more in the pipeline. Consider the following:
(1) Durable goods. September durable goods orders rose 1.8% m/m, the
largest increase in five months. Orders excluding transportation increased
0.5%, while core capital goods orders (nondefense excluding aircraft), a
key gauge of business investment, rose 0.5% and 13.5% y/y to another
record high (charts).
The major components of durable goods orders are at record highs

(chart).

### Page 7

Orders for machinery necessary to operate data centers are especially
strong (chart).
Unfilled orders for computers and electronic products rose to a record
$162.4 billion in June (chart).

### Page 8

(2) Regional business surveys. All five regional Fed manufacturing
surveys are now available for October. The regional manufacturing PMI
edged down to 13.8 from September but remained comfortably in
expansion territory, suggesting that the national ISM M-PMI likely
remained above 50.0 in October (chart).
The average prices-paid index across regional Fed surveys stood at 57.0,
near levels last seen in 2022. Meanwhile, the prices-received index
climbed to 31.6, suggesting that manufacturers are increasingly passing

higher input costs on to customers (chart).

### Page 9

II. US Economy
The industrial sector isn't the only driver of the US economy showing
remarkable strength. Another key driver of economic resilience is
consumer spending. Here is more:
(1) Consumer spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 9.7% y/y
during the week of October 16. That is well above the 2025 average of
5.8% (chart).
(2) Labor market. Consumers' perceptions of labor-market conditions
improved in October. The percentage saying jobs were plentiful rose to

26.7%, the highest since April (chart).

### Page 10

The latest weekly ADP data show private-sector employment rising by an
average of 16,750 jobs per week over the four weeks through October 10,
or roughly 67,000 jobs on a monthly basis (chart). The labor market
remains in good shape.
(3) GDP. Ahead of Thursday's release of the Q3-2026 US GDP report, the
Atlanta Fed's final GDPNow estimate suggests that the economy
expanded at a robust 3.4% annualized rate during the quarter, supported
by strong consumer spending and business investment (chart).

### Page 11

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

存儲記憶體⼤漲價造成的  PC 需求不振，外加以⾊列廠爆炸停⼯影響
數據中⼼晶⽚⽣產，造成  Intel 四季度營收，⽑利率及 EPS 都不如市

ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS

Great improvement in 3Q26

Intel reports 3Q26 sales of US$16.5bn, 2% qoq and 21% yoy, in line
with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much
higher than Bloomberg’s US$0.28 largely due to 2ppts better gross
margin, 4ppts better operating margin and non-operating income of
US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from
3Q25’s 30%. Operating margin for data center IC sales was 42%,
much higher than Bloomberg’s 27%, and improved from 2Q26’s
40% and 3Q25’s 23%.
Improve in progress for foundry business: In addition to
stronger data center IC sales, Intel foundry 3Q26 sales improved to
US$6.7bn, up 16% qoq and 58% yoy. Driven by higher utilization
rate, 3Q26 operating margin for Intel foundry was improved to -28%

### Page 2

from 2Q26’s -36% and 3Q25’s -55%, which was much better than
Bloomberg’s -36% estimates.
Source: Intel, Bloomberg est.

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS
Combined eﬀects from lack of demand on client computing (due to
memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage
inventory might extend to 1H27) and lack of data center CPU
capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

down aﬀects 2ppts sales). 4Q26 gross margin is guided to
deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).

## Document 377: OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～

Source PDF: all report pdf/OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/4 
OPEC プラスは増産見送りを継続、2027 年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 
～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラス有志７ヵ国は11 月 1 日のオンライン会合で、12 月の原油生産目標を据え置き、
３ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万バレルである。 
○ 据え置きの背景には、イラン情勢の緊迫化がある。ホルムズ海峡などで原油輸送に支障が生
じており、目標を引き上げても実際の供給増にはつながりにくい。ＩＥＡによれば、９月の
湾岸産油国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、前月から増えたものの緊迫化前の約４分
の３にとどまる。 石油製品やＬＰＧの輸出は戦前の半分以下にとどまるほか、10 月には中東
のエネルギー関連施設や船舶への攻撃も再び強まった。 
○ 供給制約により原油価格は高水準で推移し、運賃や保険料の上昇も調達コストを押し上げて
いる。 一方、 高値の長期化は世界経済を下押しし、 需要減退を招く懸念がある。ＩＥＡは 2026
年の世界石油需要を前年比で日量260 万バレル減と見込んでいる。 
○ 今後の焦点は、11 月 29 日に予定される OPEC プラス閣僚会合での 2027 年の生産枠の協議と
なる。日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産の扱いや、新たな生産基準の設定が論点だが、イラン
情勢が評価作業を複雑にしている。ＩＥＡは 2027 年に供給が日量770 万バレル増える一方、
需要の回復は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復後には需給が緩む可能性
がある。ただし、OPEC とＩＥＡの需要見通しには隔たりがあり、先行きは不透明である。 
○ 日本など輸入国にとっても、原油高の長期化は貿易収支の悪化やインフレ圧力の増幅につな
がる。当面は、OPEC プラスの会合内容、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の生産・輸出
動向、世界の石油需要を注視する必要がある。

【OPECプラス有志７ヵ国は12 月の生産目標を据え置き、３ヵ月連続で増産を見送る決定】 
OPEC（石油輸出国機構）にロシアなどの主要産油国を加えた OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジ
アラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、11 月１日
にオンライン形式で会合を開催し、12 月の原油生産目標を11 月から据え置くことを決定した。こ
れにより、３ヵ月連続で増産が見送られることとなった。 ７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万

### Page 2

2/4 
バレルとなり、９月以来の水準を維持する。今回の声明では、世界の原油市場の動向や先行きを検
討したうえで生産目標の据え置きを決定したと説明するとともに、 各国が協調減産の枠組みを順守
する姿勢をあらためて確認した。

【イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う供給制約を背景に、生産目標と実際の生産量の乖離が続いている】 
OPEC プラス有志国は、2023 年４月に発表し、 翌５月から実施した日量165 万バレルの自主減産に
ついて、 段階的な縮小を進めてきた。2026 年９月には、 この自主減産の縮小を完了したものの、 そ
の後は 10 月、11 月と生産目標を据え置いており、今回も同様の判断を下した。背景には、イラン
情勢の緊迫化を受けて中東地域からの
原油輸送に支障が生じており、 各国が生
産目標を引き上げても、 実際の供給拡大
につながりにくい状況が続いているこ
とがある（図１）。 特に、 世界有数の原油
輸送の要衝であるホルムズ海峡では、 船
舶の航行を巡る安全上の懸念が残り、 原
油輸送に制約が生じている。 湾岸産油国
は代替輸送ルートの活用などによって
輸出の回復を図っているものの、 多くの
国の原油生産量は依然として生産目標
を下回っている。 こうした状況では、 生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給拡大につながる効果は限
定的であり、有志国は現行の生産目標を維持することが妥当と判断したと考えられる。 
実際、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が公表した最新の石油市場月報によれば、９月の湾岸産油
国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、 前月から同440 万バレル増加したものの、 イラン情勢が
緊迫化する以前の水準の４分の３程度にとどまる。さらに、原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品や
ＬＰＧ（液化石油ガス）の輸出量は依然として戦前の半分以下にとどまり、供給制約の解消には至
っていない。10 月には中東地域のエネルギー関連施設や船舶を対象とする攻撃が再び強まり、 輸送
を巡る不確実性も高まっている。 このため、OPEC プラスが生産目標を引き上げても、 それが実際の
原油供給量の増加に結び付くとは限らず、 足元の供給不足を解消するうえでは、 イラン情勢の安定
化と原油輸送の正常化が不可欠といえる。

### Page 3

3/4 
【原油市場では供給制約が価格を下支えする一方、高値の長期化による需要減退も懸念】 
こうした供給制約は、国際原油市況を
押し上げている（図２）。足元の原油価格
は、イラン情勢が緊迫化する以前の水準
を大きく上回るなど、原油市場では供給
不足への警戒が根強く残っている。さら
に、原油価格の高止まりに加えて海上輸
送に伴う運賃や保険料なども上昇してお
り、原油や石油製品の調達コストを押し
上げる要因となっている。こうした状況
の下では、 仮にOPEC プラスが増産に踏み
切ったとしても、供給制約が残る限り原
油価格の大幅な下落にはつながりにくいと考えられる。 
一方、 原油価格の高止まりが世界経済の下押し圧力となることも懸念される。 原油高に伴うエネ
ルギー価格の上昇は、家計の実質購買力を低下させるとともに、企業の生産・輸送コストを押し上
げることで、個人消費や設備投資などの重しとなる。IEA は、最新の石油市場月報において、2026
年の世界の石油需要が前年比で日量260 万バレル減少するとの見通しを示しており、 供給制約に伴
う石油製品の供給不足や価格高騰などが需要を下押しすると見込んでいる。 このように需要の減退
が進めば、 将来的には原油需給が緩和する可能性もある。 そのため、OPEC プラスにとっては、足元
の供給制約への対応だけでなく、 原油高が世界経済や石油需要に及ぼす影響にも配慮した政策運営
が求められる。

【今後の焦点は2027 年の生産枠設定、供給回復に伴う需給緩和の可能性も課題】 
先行きについては、OPEC プラスが11 月29 日に予定する次回会合での協議が注目される。 今回の
有志７ヵ国による会合とは異なり、次回はOPEC プラス全体の閣僚会合が予定されており、2027 年
の生産枠を巡る協議が主要な焦点になるとみられる。OPEC プラスは、2023 年４月の自主減産につ
いては９月に縮小を完了したものの、 別途実施している日量約200 万バレルの協調減産措置は2026
年末まで維持している。2027 年以降の政策運営を巡っては、 この減産措置の取り扱いが重要な論点
となる。さらに、加盟各国の持続可能な最大生産能力に関する評価を踏まえ、新たな生産基準を設
定する方針であるものの、 イラン情勢の悪化に伴う生産設備や生産能力の拡張計画への影響などが
評価作業を複雑化させている。 
その意味では、今後の OPEC プラスの政策運営は一段と難しさを増すと予想される。イラン情勢
の緊迫化が続き、 原油輸送の制約が長期化すれば、 世界的な供給不足が続くことで原油価格には上
昇圧力が掛かりやすい。一方で、停戦協議の進展などをきっかけに原油輸送が正常化すれば、これ
まで供給が制約されてきた原油が市場に流入することにより、 需給環境が一転して緩和する可能性
がある。ＩＥＡは2027 年の世界の石油供給量が前年比で日量770 万バレル増加すると見込む一方、

需要の回復幅は同200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、 供給回復の進展に伴う需給緩和の可能
性を示唆している。もっとも、OPEC（日量１億 830 万バレル）とＩＥＡ（同１億 440 万バレル）の

ラスにとっては、供給不足が続く現状と、将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらみながら、加盟国間の利
害を調整していく必要がある。
日本をはじめとする原油輸入国にとっても、 原油需給を巡る不確実性は景気や物価の先行きを左
右する重要な要因となる。 原油価格の高止まりは、 輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支に下押し圧力を
もたらすほか、 エネルギー価格や物流コストの上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。

一方、 原油輸送の正常化によって供給量が回復すれば、 原油価格の下落を通じてインフレ圧力が和
らぐことも期待される。ただし、当面は中東情勢を巡る不透明感が根強く、原油価格は地政学的な
動向に左右されやすい状況が続くと考えられる。今後は、次回の OPEC プラス閣僚会合での協議内
容に加え、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の実際の生産・輸出動向、世界の石油需要の変化
などを注意深く見極める必要がある。

## Document 399: Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground

Source PDF: all report pdf/Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to result in a material
increase in demand for bank bonds. Securitisations beneﬁt
most directly through lower capital charges, while covered
bonds could beneﬁt modestly from the new liquidity-buﬀer
rules
Maureen Schuller
On 30 January 2027, the amendments to the Solvency II regime
will apply, making these revisions one of the important regulatory
themes for 2027. One of the key objectives of the Solvency II

reforms is to release capital and promote long-term investments by
European insurance companies in support of the ambitions of
Europe’s Savings and Investments Union (SIU). Such capital release
could also increase the capacity of insurance companies to invest
more in corporate and bank bonds.
During the past decade, insurance companies have reduced their
bond holdings in favour of equity. Rather than an active
reallocation of assets, this partially reﬂects second-round eﬀects

related to the strong equity market growth and the rise in bond

backdrop of the ECB rate hikes in 2022 and 2023. Higher yields
have also coincided in recent years with a stronger focus by banks
on issuance in shorter maturities, rather than the longer maturities
traditionally favoured by insurance companies. This has been
particularly noteworthy for covered bonds.

We don’t expect the Solvency II revisions to reverse this trend,
particularly as the amendments promote long-term equity
investments. Instead, for most bank bonds, the spread-risk capital
treatment remains broadly unchanged. Unsecured and
subordinated bank bonds continue to be treated as corporate

bonds with capital charges determined by their ratings and
duration, while qualifying covered bonds keep their preferential
treatment. The impact of the Solvency II revisions therefore arises
mainly indirectly through lower overall capital requirements, while
securitisations are the only debt instruments beneﬁting directly
through a more favourable capital treatment.
Nonetheless, certain reforms are worth highlighting for their
potential impact on insurance demand for diﬀerent bank bonds.

Among these, the amendments relating to long-term equity

### Page 3

investments, securitisations and the volatility adjustment are
particularly noteworthy.
Long-term equity investments and non-
life liquidity buffers
Under Solvency II, insurance companies can classify part of their
equity holdings as long-term equity investments (LTEI), beneﬁting
from a lower 22% capital charge. To qualify, these investments
must be held for at least ﬁve years, be listed in the EEA or OECD,
meet diversiﬁcation standards and be managed under a long-term

investment policy. The insurer must also be able to avoid forced
sales for ﬁve years, even under stressed conditions.
The Solvency II amended Delegated Regulation introduces two
approaches to demonstrate that forced equity sales can be
avoided. 1) The insurer must either have suﬃcient illiquid
homogeneous life insurance liabilities with a duration of more than
9.5 years or a liquidity buﬀer exceeding 100% for non-life insurance
obligations. 2) The insurer can also apply a forced-selling test to

prove that it can generate cash inﬂows that are consistently higher
than the cash outﬂows over ﬁve years, including under stress.
The liquidity buﬀer requirements for non-life insurance activities
are similar to the Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) requirements for
banks. The portfolio of liquid assets can comprise Level 1, Level 2A
and Level 2B assets, with the total value of Level 2A and Level 2B
assets capped at 40% and the value of the Level 2B assets at 15%.

Qualifying Level 1 assets, subject to a 0% haircut, are cash,
exposures to the ECB, multilateral development banks and
international organisations, and domestic currency exposures
to EEA central governments and central banks.
Qualifying Level 2A assets, subject to a 15% haircut, are bonds
and loans rated AA- or better that are not issued by an
insurance company or bank, and covered bonds rated AA- or
better as referred to in Article 3(1) of the European Covered

Bond Directive (CBD) (ie ‘European Covered Bonds’).
Qualifying Level 2B assets, subject to a 25% haircut, are
Simple, Transparent and Standardised (STS) securitisations (not

While unsecured bank bonds are excluded as eligible liquid assets,

the LTEI-related liquidity buﬀer approach could generate some
additional demand for covered bonds or STS securitisations of
banks. However, we don’t expect this eﬀect to be signiﬁcant given
the relatively small share of non-life insurance obligations and
insurers’ sizeable existing portfolios of other liquid assets, such as
government bonds.

The LTEI liquidity approach is separate from the generic liquidity
and concentration risk management requirements under Solvency

Lower capital requirements for
securitisations

### Page 5

The amended Solvency II Delegated Regulation lowers the capital
requirements for securitisation notes. This should encourage
insurers to invest more in these transactions, helping banks to
transfer risk and expand lending.
The risk factors (a and b ) for the spread risk of senior tranches
of STS securitisations will become fully aligned with the
treatment of similarly rated covered bonds as referred to in CBD
Article 3(1).
The risk factors for non-senior STS securitisations are reduced

to a similar extent, with the new capital charges for AAA-rated
non-senior notes at c.70% of the current capital charge, and for
AA-rated non-senior notes at 75% of the current requirement.
The diﬀerence in the capital treatment between STS and non-
STS securitisations is currently far more signiﬁcant for insurers
than for banks. This diﬀerence will be reduced substantially,
while the capital charge remains capped at 100%.
The current Solvency II regime makes no distinction between

senior and non-senior non-STS securitisation notes,
exaggerating the spread risk of senior compared with non-
senior tranches. Hence, lower risk factors are introduced for
senior non-STS compared to non-senior non-STS transactions.
As a result of these amendments, the diﬀerence in capital
requirements between CBD-compliant covered bonds and senior
STS securitisations will disappear from the end of January, while in
the highest rating buckets (AAA and AA) securitisations will be

treated better than unsecured bonds or third-country covered
bonds. Further down the rating scale, STS securitisations will be
treated closer to that of unsecured bonds from a capital
perspective, where corporate and bank unsecured bonds will still
beneﬁt from a more favourable capital charge than similarly rated
senior STS securitisations.
Securitisation alternatives will become
more competitive from a capital perspective

### Page 6

Source: European Commission, ING
European insurance companies currently hold only about €37bn in
collateralised debt, such as asset-backed securities (ABS) and
residential mortgage-backed securities (RMBS). This is only 12% of
what they hold in covered bonds and 3% of their corporate debt
portfolio.
The Solvency II amendments may motivate insurance companies
to diversify their debt portfolios a bit further away from covered
bonds and other bank bond products towards securitisations.

However, the impact is not expected to be substantial given that
the weighted average lives of securitisation notes may not always
be well suited to the longer-term investment needs of insurance
companies.
Volatility adjustment to align spread
sensitivities more closely
Insurance companies calculate the best estimate of their liabilities
by discounting the expected future liability cash ﬂows using the
relevant risk-free interest rate term structure. To mitigate the

balance sheet impact of bond spread volatility (ie, on the asset side
of their balance sheet), insurance companies can discount their
liabilities at the risk-free rate plus a volatility adjustment (VA).
The volatility adjustment increases the
discount rate

### Page 7

Spot rate, %
Source: EIOPA, ING
For each relevant currency (or national market in the case of the
euro area), the volatility adjustment represents 65% of the spread
(S) between the interest rate on the reference portfolio and the
relevant risk-free interest rate, adjusted for a risk correction (RC). The
latter correction factor removes the part of the spread that is
attributable to expected losses or unexpected credit or other risks
to the assets. The volatility adjustment is only added to the

applicable risk-free interest rates that are not derived from
extrapolation.
For each relevant country, a country-speciﬁc adjustment may also
be added to the volatility adjustment before the application of the
65% factor if a) the risk-corrected country spread is more than twice
the risk-corrected currency spread, and b) the risk-corrected country
spread is more than 85bp. The country component in the volatility
adjustment helps mitigate excessive widening in speciﬁc countries.

Under the current regime, the representative currency and country
portfolios are constructed as uniform portfolios, even though
insurers’ actual ﬁxed-income portfolios may diﬀer substantially.
Some insurers can therefore beneﬁt excessively from the volatility
adjustment if the resulting reduction in the best estimate of their
liabilities exceeds the decline in the value of the relevant assets
when credit spreads widen.

### Page 8

For this reason, the Solvency II revisions change the calculation of
the volatility adjustment by a) making the applicable risk
corrections (RC) more spread-sensitive, and b) introducing a credit
spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR) to better align the volatility
adjustment with the insurer’s asset-liability proﬁle. The latter ratio
reﬂects the price value of a basis point of the insurer’s bond,
securitisation and loan investments versus the price value of a

basis point of the best estimate of liabilities, with a minimum of 0
and a maximum of 1.
Moreover, subject to approval by the supervisory authority, an
undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment (USA) of up to 105% may be
applied to align the risk-corrected spread on the reference portfolio
more closely with that of the insurer’s own portfolio. This must not
lead to an increase in the applied risk-adjusted spread for more
than two consecutive quarters. Moreover, when the USA is used, the

insurer cannot also increase the volatility adjustment through a
macro volatility adjustment.
The macro volatility adjustment replaces the existing country-
speciﬁc adjustment and can be added to the euro volatility
adjustment when the relevant conditions are met. It aims to
mitigate the impact of excessive spread widening in individual euro
area countries and is based on the diﬀerence between the risk-
corrected euro spread and the risk-corrected spread for the relevant

country.
Volatility adjustment calculations

Subscripts cu = currency, co = country, ma = macro; VA = volatility
adjustment, S = spread of the reference portfolio, RC = risk correction, RCS
= risk-corrected spread of the reference portfolio, CSSR = credit spread
sensitivity ratio, USA = undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment.
Source: European Commission, ING
The introduction of additional minimum conditions, such as
supervisory approval and the availability of adequate processes to
calculate the volatility adjustment, has also facilitated the upward

revision of the general application ratio (GAR) from 65% to 85%.
Primarily through the introduction of the credit spread sensitivity
ratio (CSSR), the volatility adjustment will better reﬂect the
relationship between each insurer’s asset and liability sensitivity to
changes in the volatility adjustment. The revision may provide
modest support for bonds that increase the asset portfolio’s spread
sensitivity, as a higher CSSR results in a larger volatility adjustment

until the ratio reaches its cap of one. The revised calculations should
also reduce the possibility for some insurers to beneﬁt
disproportionately from the volatility adjustment when their assets
are less spread-sensitive than the reference portfolio.
Conclusion
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to trigger a material
reallocation in favour of bank bonds. Securitisations are the most
obvious beneﬁciaries of a more favourable capital treatment, while

covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should mainly beneﬁt
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers. We

## Document 434: US Equity Views_ AI capex, required revenues, and what's priced in the AI trade

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Equity Views_ AI capex, required revenues, and what's priced in the AI trade.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

An update on AI and US equities

Both stocks and investor views relating to the AI trade have been exceptionally 
volatile during the last few months. Most of the volatility has been concentrated in the 
AI infrastructure complex, but the latest earnings season also drove sharp moves in the 
hyperscalers and among software stocks. 
Below we address three of the most common AI-related questions we have received 
from clients in recent weeks: 
1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
2. What revenues are required to generate a sufficient return on recent AI 
investments? 
3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing?

1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
The largest US hyperscalers are on track to spend $800 billion in capex in 2026 and 
consensus estimates imply they will spend $1.1 trillion in 2027. Consensus 2027 
capex estimates have been revised higher by $175 billion since the start of the Q2 
earnings season. Commentary from the hyperscalers during earnings season described a 
supply-constrained environment and large revenue backlogs, and these factors are 
driving the companies to tap both debt and equity markets to support additional 
investment spending. Based on consensus estimates, capex in 2027 is on track to reach a

### Page 3

larger share of GDP than any technological investment cycle since the railroad build-out 
in the late 1800’s. 
Our baseline forecast is that hyperscaler capex spending will increase substantially 
in 2027 but that the rate of both capex growth and upside surprises to consensus 
estimates will diminish relative to recent quarters. GS equity analysts estimate that 
hyperscaler capex will register $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028. Their 
estimates are $130 billion and $100 billion above the consensus estimates, respectively, 
but nonetheless imply a deceleration in capex growth from nearly 100% in 2026 to 54% 
in 2027 and 12% in 2028.

The need for external financing and potential supply bottlenecks will likely 
constrain the rate of capex growth next year. Hyperscaler capex spending now 
exceeds cash flow from operations, which means large further growth will increasingly 
need funding via debt and equity issuance. While hyperscalers have added leverage, 
their balance sheets do not appear to be a binding constraint and their interest coverage 
ratios remain extremely healthy. Our credit strategists instead point to concentration in 
the investment grade market as the more relevant bottleneck, which will require 
additional funding from private credit and equity issuance. Potential government 
restrictions on data center construction or an intentional slowdown in frontier model 
roll-out could also act as potential constraints on spending, as could shortages of labor, 
power, memory, or other inputs. Constraints on capex represent a risk to the near-term 
earnings of AI infrastructure beneficiaries, but could help ease investor concerns about 
overbuilding.

### Page 4

Going forward, token demand growth will need to outpace declining token prices to 
support continued growth in investment spending. Frontier models are currently a 
key source of demand for hyperscaler compute. However, the rise of competitive 
open-source models has contributed to a decline in average token prices. Measures of 
frontier token demand slowed in July, simultaneous with sell-offs of many AI 
infrastructure stocks, but accelerated again in August. 
For most AI infrastructure companies, decelerating AI investment spending 
suggests decelerating EPS growth. AI infrastructure companies, and semis in 
particular, have been the clearest beneficiaries of the capex surge. Continued upward 
revisions to consensus 2027 capex estimates should support continued positive 
revisions to consensus 2027 EPS estimates for many AI infrastructure stocks, but the 
magnitude of revisions will likely diminish relative to what occurred in H1 2026.

### Page 5

2. What revenues are required to generate a return on AI investments? 
Our equity analysts estimate that the hyperscalers would need to generate annual 
AI revenues of roughly $300 billion in the next few years to break even on their 
average annual capex investments in 2026 and 2027. These estimates reflect their 
expectations for average annual AI capex in 2026 and 2027 for the US hyperscalers, 
including AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL, and SPCX. The breakeven revenues 
required for this capex represent the sum of annual depreciation and operating 
expenses. Exhibit 8 provides an estimated sensitivity of required AI revenues the 
hyperscalers must generate in different return on invested capital (“ROIC”) scenarios. 
These required revenue estimates are anchored on average annual capex in 2026 and 
2027 and would argue for even larger estimates based on cumulative capex.

lines represent equal-weighted averages of growth implied by Anera and OpenRouter data

### Page 6

The hyperscalers’ current estimated AI revenues remain below the amount that 
would allow them to break even on their capex, but those revenues are growing 
quickly and revenue backlogs are sizable. The hyperscalers have started to monetize 
their AI investments. Year/year cloud revenue growth for AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT, and 
ORCL has accelerated from 25% in 2024 to 48% in Q2 2026. In dollar terms, annualized 
cloud revenues in excess of their 2024 growth rate registered nearly $70 billion in Q2 
and have been roughly doubling during the past few quarters. The three major US public 
cloud companies (AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT) have also announced a combined revenue 
backlog of $1.7 trillion, which provides line of sight into these companies’ ability to meet 
required revenue targets.

operating expenses, and return on invested capital using gross capex, based on

Economy Needed to Justify ROIC on Hyperscaler AI Capex for more details.

### Page 7

The magnitude of capex in 2026 and 2027 points to even larger “required” annual 
revenues in the next few years for the “application layer” of companies buying 
much of that compute from the hyperscalers. These revenues reflect the additional 
costs and profit margins of application companies on top of the “required” hyperscaler 
revenues selling compute. EBIT margins for large-cap software companies have 
averaged 40% during the past five years. We utilize EBIT margins rather than gross 
margins to include the costs of both training and inference, and assume additional 
expenses alongside compute. The range of potential required revenues is wide 
depending on which companies constitute the application layer and how their costs are 
structured.  
Exhibit 11 provides a range of potential “required” revenues based on hyperscaler 
ROIC and application layer EBIT margins. For example, the application layer would 
have to generate over $1 trillion in annual revenues if those companies had a 30% EBIT 
margin and the hyperscalers generated a 10%-20% return on invested capital. 
The “required” application layer revenues that would justify current capex are large 
but potentially achievable. For example, estimates of global advertising and software 
spending each equal roughly $1.5 trillion in 2026. Our economists estimate that total 
annual AI US labor productivity gains that will potentially accrue to capital total roughly 
$1.8 trillion. However, not all of these gains will necessarily accrue to the infrastructure 
and application layer companies. In addition, another year of strong capex growth in 
2028 would raise the hurdle further. AI-related revenues and capex needs would be 
substantially larger accounting for physical AI or the potential emergence of AGI.

### Page 8

3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing? 
While market participants express skepticism about the durability of AI 
infrastructure stocks’ earnings growth, that view is at least partially priced. The 
median AI infrastructure stock has de-rated from 32x consensus forward earnings in 
April 2026 to 22x today. In addition to tracking standard P/E multiples, comparing 
recent changes in market cap to recent changes in earnings can signal how long the 
market expects current earnings strength to persist. Among various AI infrastructure 
baskets, that ratio remains most elevated among optical networking, electrical grid, and 
liquid cooling stocks while expectations appear most conservative among 
semiconductors and utilities. 
Semiconductor valuations appear to embed particularly skeptical views regarding 
the sustainability of current earnings strength. Valuations have declined sharply 
among Semis stocks, which is typical among cyclical companies with strong near-term 
growth but uncertain medium-term growth. We believe the investment opportunity is 
more compelling in fabless chip stocks than in memory stocks. Our equity analysts are 
Buy-rated on NVDA and AVGO but Neutral-rated on MU.

### Page 9

Memory stock prices imply earnings will decline in coming years, but the degree of 
pessimism does not appear particularly extreme relative to past cycles. Memory 
stocks currently trade at a 2-year forward P/E of 4x, half their 15-year average, but 
Memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double their 
historical average. To bring the current P/E of memory stocks to its 15-year average, 
earnings in coming years would need to decline by roughly 50%. While this decline is 
large in absolute terms, memory is a cyclical industry and has experienced larger EPS 
declines, including outright negative earnings, during previous cycles. This pricing will 
prove too pessimistic if 2028 forecasts are realized, but many investors have expressed 
skepticism about rotating back into higher beta pockets of the infrastructure trade as 
capex growth begins to normalize.

### Page 10

In contrast, the fabless chip stocks also trade at discounted valuations but have had 
less extreme margin expansion. Margins for the largest fabless chip stocks are only 
modestly above their historical average. Earnings in the next few years would need to 
decline by about 10% to bring the P/E back to its historical average. This is similar to the 
magnitude of historical realized cyclical earnings declines for the group.

The likelihood of decelerating capex growth should be a positive catalyst for the 
hyperscalers. We previously laid out three conditions for the hyperscalers to regain 
leadership. These include decelerating macroeconomic growth, demonstrated 
monetization of AI investment, and decelerating capex growth. Our economists believe 
US economic growth is no longer accelerating. The hyperscalers have already started to 
demonstrate signs of monetization, and we expect capex growth will slow in 2027. 
Uncertainty about the length, magnitude, and return on investment for this capex cycle 
will likely persist. However, the hyperscalers have de-rated to their lowest P/E multiples 
in over a decade, and the group now trades just four turns above the median S&P 500 
company, its smallest premium on record. While investors have recently been focused 
more on the hyperscalers’ negative free cash flows than on their earnings, visibility into 
improving FCF as revenues accelerate and capex decelerates should be supportive for 
the stocks.

### Page 11

We expect the progression of the AI trade towards application roll-out will remain a 
source of return dispersion. We believe the “winners” and “losers” from the application 
layer will take several quarters to play out, as AI applications get built and adoption 
broadens. 
The threat of disruption had weighed on the share prices of many Software stocks 
earlier this year, but the group has rebounded sharply in recent weeks. Reported 
Software fundamentals have shown little sign of AI disruption, with annual recurring 
revenue growth for the median stock in GS analyst coverage accelerating slightly in 
recent quarters. A group of Software stocks has rallied by 45% and is now within 8% of 
its high reached in late 2025. Software stocks focused on IT infrastructure and 
cybersecurity have posted the strongest returns, while enterprise management software 
remain below their previous highs.

### Page 12

Consumer-facing businesses with high switching costs have also recently emerged 
as a potential area of disruption. The increasing use of agents, including by new 
consumer-facing tools such as Muse, may disrupt the business models of some 
consumer firms. Our colleagues in Global Banking & Markets developed a basket of 
“consumer inertia” stocks (GSXUSWCH) where user stickiness is driven more by effort 
required to take action rather than product differentiation. The basket includes 
insurance, telecom, travel, subscriptions, marketplaces, and fintech. The basket has 
lagged a sector-neutral hedge by 5 pp since mid-September.

### Page 13

Enterprise AI adoption remains in early stages but spending has recently 
accelerated, suggesting the impact of AI on corporate earnings should grow 
increasingly visible in coming quarters. The Census Bureau survey suggests that 40% 
of large US firms are using AI in the production of goods and services. Our analysis of Q2 
earnings commentary suggests roughly 50% of S&P 500 companies are using AI in the 
context of productivity, but very few companies have quantified the productivity uplift 
today. However, the revenue run rates of model providers and alternative measures of 
company spending on AI have accelerated during the last several months alongside the 
increasing use of agentic harnesses. 
In contrast with past periods of market exuberance, the equity market today does 
not appear to be pricing expectations of widespread productivity gains. Baskets of 
AI productivity beneficiaries have traded roughly sideways on a relative basis YTD, 
matching the trajectory of near-term forward EPS estimates. Similarly, the measure of 
market-implied long-term growth expectations in Exhibit 22 is only slightly above the 
historical average (for details, see US Equity Views: The next phases of the AI trade).

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
Fiji’s resilience is being tested...
Fiji’s economy entered 2026 with growth already normalising
from the post-pandemic recovery. Real GDP growth slowed from
3.6% in 2024 to 2.5% in 2025. Foreign reserves started the year

around $3.7bn, banking-system liquidity remained high and the
RBF maintained its Overnight Policy Rate at 0.25%.
Activity so far into 2026 has been somewhat stronger than what
we feared. Visitor arrivals reached 762,352 during January–
September, up 3.7%yr, while construction, investment lending,
cement sales, remittances and private-sector credit remained
firm. Nevertheless, Westpac Fiji has kept its 2026 growth
forecast unchanged at 2.0%. Stronger headline indicators are

not translating one-for-one into real economic growth because
higher prices and intermediate costs are absorbing a growing
share of nominal activity.
The outlook changed materially in March as the Middle East
conflict disrupted global energy markets. Fiji remains heavily
dependent on imported petroleum products, so higher refined-
fuel, freight and insurance costs flowed through transport,
electricity, food distribution and wider business expenses. By

June, motor spirit price reached $3.93/litre and diesel at a record
$4.58/litre, while automotive-fuel sales value rose 24.4%yr in the
June quarter.
As this report goes out, the Middle East conflict remains a
key external downside risk. At least 12 vessels were attacked
in the week ending 16 October and yet it is reported that oil
flows through Hormuz averaged 11.6mn barrels per day to 13
October, about 68% of pre-war levels through alternative and

costly routes. Iran says a seven-day reopening proposal remains
available, but US-Iran negotiations remain unresolved.
Westpac Economics’ most recent scenario analysis “ Persistent
energy pressures have further to run ” indicates that relief will
be gradual. Brent crude is forecast to average US$101/bbl in Q4
2026 before easing through 2027. Refined products are expected
to normalise more slowly because refinery capacity, inventories,
freight and insurance remain constrained. Under Westpac’s

escalation scenario, in a return to open conflict Brent could reach
around US$150/bbl in H1 2027.
Domestic tourism sector demonstrates the nominal-versus-
real growth tension. June-quarter visitor arrivals increased to
287,588, yet earnings fell 4.9%yr to $708.1mn. First-half tourism
earnings declined 1.7%yr despite higher arrivals, even though
hotel takings increased 9.5%yr.
Australia and New Zealand remain critical, together contributing
around 71% of September arrivals. Westpac expects Australian

growth to slow from 2.1% in 2026 to 1.5% in 2027, while New
Zealand strengthens from 1.9% to 2.6%. Tourism therefore
increasingly needs to focus on yield rather than visitor numbers
alone.
Shamal Chand
Senior Economist, Westpac Pacific Economics

### Page 3
